the negative control each concept row carries:
  typcla           5a   make `::CLASS ... METACLASS` a no-op, so the declared class is an instance of `.Class` like a plain one. The section's abstract kind has no control here and is not owed one: this probe asks each class its `~class~id`, which an abstract class answers exactly as a plain one does, and abstract enforcement is `abscla`'s row, at 5b because the check lives inside `~new` -- a discriminator added here would duplicate that row rather than cover a gap
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: the four kinds themselves are 5a, with enforcement split into its own rows
  objcla           5b   rebuild an existing instance's method lookup from its class on every send, so a method defined after the instance was created answers -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: the section's distinguishing claim is that an object acquires its class's methods at the time of its creation, which needs `~new`; the plan's handover puts instance construction in 5b
  xmixin           5a   answer `~baseClass` with the class itself rather than with the mixin's first non-mixin superclass, or merge an INHERIT target's methods in front of the class's own
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: a mixin's base class, and who may inherit it, is 5a
  abscla           5b   drop `checkAbstract` from the `~new` path, so an abstract class constructs -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: abstract-*class* enforcement is 5b, because the check lives inside `~new`; the abstract-*method* half is 5a and has no arm of this section's probe
  xmetac           5a   ignore `METACLASS`, so a class declared with one is still an instance of `.Class`
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: the metaclass graph and its circularity is 5a
  xcremet          5a   install a quoted `::CLASS` identifier under a different name, or give the new class a superclass other than Object
      owning-phase authority: no row in the spec enumeration; the probe needs `::CLASS` install with a quoted identifier and a `::METHOD ... CLASS` body
  usingcl          5a   answer `~subclass` with a class whose superclass is Object rather than the receiver
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: `mixinClass()` and `inherit()` are 5a, and `~subclass` is the same factory protocol reached by message rather than by directive
  xscope           5a   answer `~method` from the flattened all-scopes dictionary, so an inherited name answers where the oracle raises
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: the method dictionary's one entry per scope, and `Method~scope`, are 5a
  usesem           5b   put a `setMethod` definition in the class's dictionary rather than in the object's own scope, so it reaches the class's other instances -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: per-object methods -- SETMETHOD and ENHANCED, and the scope they create -- are 5b
  methna           5a   stop uppercasing a method name as it is added, so a name defined in lower case is not found by the message that names it. Both sites have to go together, measured: `dispatch/class_protocol.rs`'s own `method_name_pair`, and `MethodDict::replace_method`. Either one alone leaves this row `agree` and the corpus untouched
      owning-phase authority: no row in the spec enumeration; the probe needs `~define` and `~method`, which the enumeration files under 5a
  xmeths           5a   search a superclass before the class's own dictionary, or drop the `UNKNOWN` step so a miss goes straight to NOMETHOD
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: the complete method search order is 5a with the per-object arm 5b; this probe deliberately omits that arm, which is `usesem`'s row
  unkno            5a   **delete the `UNKNOWN` step** and record that this row reddens. One of D50's two required controls; runnable only once the step exists
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: the complete method search order, of which `UNKNOWN` is a step, is 5a
  chsrod           5a   start a scope-override send at the receiver's own class rather than at the named scope, which makes `self~type:super` recurse or answer the subclass's method
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: changing the search order -- `~m:scope`, `~m:super` -- is 5a
  pubpri           5a   let a PRIVATE method answer a send from outside the object, so the outside-send line answers instead of raising 97.2
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: PUBLIC / PACKAGE / PRIVATE as three access scopes is 5a
  creo             5b   stop calling `init` after `~new` builds the object, or pass `~new`'s arguments to the wrong `init` -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: initialization of an instance -- `~new`, `init`, `self~init:super` -- is 5b
  obdes            5b   skip the termination sweep, since under D59 a class object's storage is never reclaimed and no collection ever reaches one. Measured as run: the row reddens **silently**, rc 0 with empty stderr on both sides, differing on stdout alone -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: object destruction and uninitialization -- `UNINIT` and its propagation flags -- is 5b, with the flags carried by 5a's code
  reqstr           5a   **delete the `makeString` limb** and record that this row reddens (the second of D50's two required controls), and **answer `makeString` with the wrong string**, which reddens the same row at rc 0 with empty stderr on both sides -- that second one is table C's mutation 3
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: required string values -- request("STRING") to makeString to NOSTRING to defaultName -- is 5a
  concurr          5a   refuse `REPLY` inside a method body, or stop returning its expression to the sender
      owning-phase authority: spec enumeration: GUARDED/UNGUARDED, REPLY and GUARD legality is 5a, with the semantics Phase 6's
  classmeth        5a   drop a class from the registry, so its `~id` line cannot answer
      owning-phase authority: no row in the spec enumeration; the probe asks `~id` of every class reachable as an environment symbol
  chi              5a   answer `~superClass` with the whole superclass list's last element rather than the class's direct superclass
      owning-phase authority: no row in the spec enumeration as a section; the hierarchy list is the wiring rows' own authority and the probe asks `~superClass` and `~superClasses`
  methodsbyclass   5b   route `matrix[2, 3] = 0` to a single-index `[]=`, so the element read back is not the one written; or transpose the index mapping, which the `order` line reads and no write-then-read pair can. Transpose the **offset accumulation alone**, leaving `multi_dimension_position`'s bounds pass pairing each subscript with its own dimension: reversing that pairing does not terminate, because a subscript past the dimension it was mispaired with re-enters `array_extend_multi` and the interpreter thread overflows its stack at rc 134, which reads as an infrastructure failure and not as a reddened row -- 5b
      owning-phase authority: no row in the spec enumeration; the probe needs a multidimensional Array instance for the section's own `matrix[2, 3] = 0` note, and the plan's handover puts instance construction in 5b
